# Skills · P · part 25

- **pdlc**: End-to-end delivery from a Confluence page or Jira ticket to a pull request: read requirements, spec, Figma design (or a proposal), plan, Jira tickets, TDD build, verify, PR. Use when the user gives a Confluence or Jira link and wants it built, or says /pdlc or resume. ([siddeshthoratrsystem/conversational_ai](https://github.com/siddeshthoratrsystem/conversational_ai/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/pdlc))
- **pdlc-add-app**: 在已有项目里加一个前端应用（先探测布局，跟随现有目录与技术栈）；新项目一次性初始化用 /pdlc-bootstrap ([kanfu-panda/pdlc-skills](https://github.com/kanfu-panda/pdlc-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pdlc-add-app) · ★18)
- **pdlc-add-service**: 在已有项目里加一个后端服务（先探测布局，跟随现有目录与技术栈）；新项目一次性初始化用 /pdlc-bootstrap ([kanfu-panda/pdlc-skills](https://github.com/kanfu-panda/pdlc-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pdlc-add-service) · ★18)

- **pdlc-autopilot**: THE primary entry point for all SDLC and PDLC work. Use when user mentions: "SDLC", "PDLC", "build this feature", "implement the spec", "run the SDLC loop", "go back to SDLC", "continue implementation", "product context", "document this feature", "launch prep", or any spec-related work (requirements, design, tasks, implementation). This skill ORCHESTRATES Kiro skills (kiro:spec-*) as sub-operations, wrapped with product phases (P0 product context, P1 product skeptic, P2 docs, P3 demo & packaging). ⚠️ DO NOT invoke kiro:spec-* skills directly when user wants SDLC/PDLC workflow! Always use this… ([vishnujayvel/pdlc-autopilot](https://github.com/vishnujayvel/pdlc-autopilot/tree/HEAD/templates/skills/pdlc-autopilot) · ★6)

- **pdxui-display**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) display atoms — button, button-group, icon, badge, chip, avatar, divider, kbd, label, progress, spinner, image, card, accordion, carousel — with props and events. Use when placing a basic visual element in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-display))
- **pdxui-forms**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) form-building components — pdx-form, auto-form, form-template, json-editor, form-field/section/actions, fieldset, field-group/list, input-group, inline-edit, wizard — with props and events. Use when building a form, an edit screen or a wizard in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-forms))
- **pdxui-i18n**: PDX (@pdxui) internationalization: $t, $n, $d and $r, setting up and switching locales, translating the strings the built-in components render, reacting to a locale change, right-to-left. Use when a .pdx app is not English-only. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-i18n))
- **pdxui-infra**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) infrastructure components — pdx-provide (a DI/context provider) and pdx-error-boundary — with props and events. Use when wiring the plumbing of a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-infra))
- **pdxui-inputs**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) input controls — text, number, masked, password, search and rich-text inputs, checkbox, radio, switch, toggle, segmented, select, autocomplete, tree-select, cascader, date/time/color pickers, slider, rating, tags, file upload. Use when adding a form control to a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-inputs))
- **pdxui-language**: The .pdx language: the file format (template, script, style scoped), the runes ($signal, $derived, $effect), the declarations (@prop, @page, @event and more), the template syntax and how a project is wired. Use before writing or editing any .pdx file, or when starting a PDX app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-language))
- **pdxui-layout**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) layout and shell components — pdx-app-layout, pdx-app-shell, pdx-page-header, navbar, sidebar, splitter, scroll-area, row/col grid, aspect-ratio, masonry, toolbar, affix, scroll-spy — with props and events. Use when building the page structure or app shell of a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-layout))
- **pdxui-navigation**: PDX navigation components — router-outlet, link, nav-menu, bottom-nav, breadcrumb, menu, menubar, dropdown and context menus, command palette, tabs, pagination, fab, split-button — with props and events. Use when adding links, navigation, menus, tabs or a command palette to a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-navigation))
- **pdxui-overlay**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) overlay and feedback components — dialog, alert-dialog, drawer, edit-drawer, relation-picker, bottom-sheet, popover, tooltip, toast, banner, block-ui — with props and events. Use when showing a dialog, drawer, popover, toast or loading overlay in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-overlay))
- **pdxui-routing**: PDX (@pdxui/router) routing: @page, params, @guard and where a denial goes, @loader, layouts and nested routes, one chunk per page and prefetching, keep-alive, the query string, navigation hooks, @title/@meta and scroll restoration. Use when a .pdx app has more than one page. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-routing))
- **pdxui-screens**: Compose a screen that reads as a product, not a demo: grid or cards, what the header and sidebar carry, empty, loading and error states, what never to rebuild by hand. Use when laying out a screen or an app in .pdx, after picking components (pdxui) and the brand (pdxui-theme). ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-screens))
- **pdxui-setup**: PDX (@pdxui) project setup and build: installing, the Vite plugin, the pdx CLI (dev, build, check, new, analyze, theme, i18n), dev interpreted vs production compiled, first-screen preloading, CDN and framework interop, devtools. Use when starting, building or shipping a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-setup))
- **pdxui-testing**: PDX (@pdxui) testing and permissions: mount, queries, tick and events from @pdxui/core/testing, contract tests; who the user is (createAuthStore), setPermissions, gating UI and routes, what survives a reload. Use when testing .pdx components or gating a .pdx app by permission. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-testing))
- **pdxui-theme**: Theme and brand a PDX app: generate a WCAG-AA theme from one brand color with `npx pdx theme`, pick one of the 13 shipped themes, use the semantic tokens, dark mode and density. Use when branding an app, editing a theme, choosing a token, or when a theme does not apply. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-theme))
- **pdxui-validation**: PDX (@pdxui) forms and validation: @form schemas, field binding, field arrays, a rule across two fields, when to save, one form split across components, several forms under one save. Use when a .pdx form must validate, save, or span several components. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-validation))

- **peak-end-rule**: Activate when: user says 'how will they remember this,' 'experience design,' 'journey design,' 'memorable moment,' 'end-of-experience,' or 'our NPS is lower than expected'; when designing or auditing a multi-stage customer or user journey; when a competitor with similar quality earns higher recommendation rates. Do NOT activate when: the interaction is instantaneous with no temporal sequence (single API call, one-tap action); or when total real-time utility matters more than retrospective memory (welfare assessments, health measurements). More: deciqai.com/s/peak-end-rule ([deciqAI/knowledge-skills](https://github.com/deciqAI/knowledge-skills/tree/HEAD/peak-end-rule) · ★16)

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/peak-overlap-analysis-across-algorithms))

- **peak-parameter-recovery-validation**: Use when validating that a peak-finding/peak-fitting function recovers correct peak positions, intensities, and widths — e.g. testing find_peaks_1d_gauss on synthetic 1D signals before applying it to real chromatographic or IMS data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/peak-parameter-recovery-validation))

- **pearson-correlation-computation**: Use when you have two per-droplet numeric metrics (e.g., DIEM score.debris and percent spliced reads) aligned on shared droplet/barcode identifiers and need to quantify their linear agreement, expecting a high positive R (~0.7-0.99). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-computation--75bb3a5e185e79a3))
- **pearson-correlation-computation**: Use when you have two condition-specific expression matrices (rows = gene symbols, columns = sample IDs) and need per-patient differential co-expression edges for downstream WMDS.net driver-node ranking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-computation--2125dbc3e5291a82))
- **pearson-correlation-computation**: Use when validating that CLEAR-derived FPB and CIRCscore values from ribo− paired-end RNA-seq reproduce published Pearson correlations against FPKM/FPM or RT-qPCR measurements. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-computation--4680dee8866dc7d3))
- **pearson-correlation-computation-across-tools**: Use when you have quantification results from two or more independent implementations, versions, or variants of the same analysis tool (e.g. C++ salmon 1.11. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v1/skills/pearson-correlation-computation-across-tools))
- **pearson-correlation-cutoff-network-filtering**: Use when you have a PCC-scored functional association network (e.g. from FAVA on scRNA-seq or proteomics data) and need to extract a high-confidence subnetwork at STRING-style probability cutoffs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-cutoff-network-filtering))
- **pearson-correlation-deconvolution-assessment**: Use when simulated bulk HiChIP mixtures have known subpopulation-specific loop counts and you must quantify deconvolution accuracy or benchmark a model-based inference against a simple plug-in baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-deconvolution-assessment))
- **pearson-correlation-distance-computation**: Use when you need a continuous cell ordering (pseudo-time) from a binary/transformed eeSNV matrix: Pearson correlation distance between cells feeds a minimum spanning tree that must recapitulate a primary-to-metastasis trajectory and expose subgroup ramifications. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-distance-computation))
- **pearson-correlation-distance-preservation-analysis**: Use when evaluating whether a 2D embedding of high-dimensional single-cell (CyTOF/scRNA-seq) data retains global inter-cluster geometry, using manually annotated cell-population labels and centroid distance concordance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-distance-preservation-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-expression-interaction-analysis**: Use when a cell×gene interaction-score matrix (e.g., MAPIT scores) is co-profiled with single-cell transcriptome data and you need per-gene correlation of binding strength with expression across subpopulations defined by cell cycle or clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-expression-interaction-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-association**: Use when an untargeted lipidomics feature table has few biological groups (e.g. resistant vs parent strains, ~4 replicates each) and you need a feature-level association ranking to complement multivariate PCA/PLS-DA cluster separation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-association))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-collapsing**: Use when a two-condition metabolomics feature table contains redundant or highly correlated metabolites (e.g. |Pearson r| &gt; 0.7) or co-classed compounds (e.g. BCAAs, fatty acids) that should be collapsed into single nodes before joint network estimation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-collapsing))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-mask-analysis**: Use when a registered multimodal MSI dataset has a thresholded binary pixel annotation (e.g. Ly6G IFM segmentation mask) and the goal is to identify the molecular signature — such as ether-linked PCs like PC O-36:4 — of a specific cell population. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-mask-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-ranking**: Use when you have a trained VAE/latent-space MSI classifier (e.g. massNet Code layer output) plus decoded per-m/z spatial images and a pixel-level class-probability map, and need to identify which m/z peaks drive tumor delineation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-ranking))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-transformation**: Use when preparing a scRNA-seq expression matrix (CSV) plus class labels for graph-embedding visualization with GraphDDP/graph_embed, after class filtering, normalization, and feature selection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-transformation))
- **pearson-correlation-gene-ranking**: Use when you have scPagwas per-cell gPAS scores for a GWAS trait and a normalized scRNA-seq matrix, and need to derive a ranked trait-relevant gene set (e.g., top 1,000 genes) for downstream enrichment or gene-set comparisons. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-gene-ranking))
- **pearson-correlation-gene-selection**: Use when imputing dropout-affected zero entries in an scRNA-seq expression matrix and you must choose which genes from a pre-defined gene subspace serve as regression predictors for a target gene. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-gene-selection))
- **pearson-correlation-grn-estimation**: Use when benchmarking a cell-specific network inference method against dyngen's per-cell ground-truth regulatory networks and you need a simple correlation-differencing baseline (SSN) using Pearson correlation as the base estimator. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-grn-estimation))
- **pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering**: Use when clustering a log2-transformed, median-centered gene expression matrix (samples × transcripts) with a Pearson-correlation-distance hierarchical method, and when you need stable cluster assignments across K values to compare against an alternative clustering method. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering))
- **pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering**: Use when you have deep features from a trained DeepCC model and need to test whether features self-organize into biological groups (e.g., three groups mapping to CMS1 MSI, CMS3 metabolic, CMS4 EMT). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering))
- **pearson-correlation-imputation-evaluation**: Use when comparing gene imputation methods on paired observed/reference matrices (e.g. SERM vs MAGIC, SAVER, scVI on the cellular taxonomy dataset) and you need a quantitative accuracy score per sampling efficiency. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-imputation-evaluation))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when a feature table of variant-prioritization predictor scores (e.g., the 574 dsQTL-positive and 27,735 negative SNPs of training dataset 1) must be screened for collinearity before feeding scores into ncDeepBrain or gene prioritization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when validating that a per-cell background/noise estimate (µ1) captures shared variance with isotype controls in CITE-seq ADT data, or when quantifying concordance of fitted parameters between alternative mixture-component definitions (k=2 vs k=3). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation--202e36b42f605555))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when a supervised cell annotation pipeline must decide between model architectures based on cross-cell-type similarity, or when a K x K cell-type correlation matrix from averaged scRNA-seq expression profiles is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation--61116901fd336189))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-construction**: Use when a single-cell isoform-level count matrix is too sparse for reliable cell-level Pearson correlation, and a noise-robust, cell-type-summarized co-expression matrix is needed as input to isoform clustering (e.g. acorde semi-automated clustering). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-construction))
- **pearson-correlation-meta-analysis**: Use when the same feature/gene pairs have Pearson correlation coefficients measured across multiple independent studies (each with its own sample size) and they must be combined into a single estimate with fixed or random effects weighting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-meta-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-method-comparison**: Use when multiple gene-set activity scoring methods (e.g. ORIGINS2 network activity, AUCell, mean expression) have been run on the same gene sets and you need to quantify their agreement with Pearson correlations and summary means. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-method-comparison))
- **pearson-correlation-model-comparison**: Use when comparing CNN, HyenaDNA, or Caduceus variants against an expert model on base-pair-resolution regression of transcription initiation signals over 100,000-bp sequences in DNALONGBENCH. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-model-comparison))
- **pearson-correlation-of-alignment-functions**: Use when comparing or validating the retention-time shift correction produced by MetMatch against an independent alignment (e.g., XCMS) on LC-HRMS feature lists, to confirm both tools recovered consistent non-linear Rt shifts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-alignment-functions))
- **pearson-correlation-of-count-matrices**: Use when two raw gene-barcode count matrices produced by different scRNA-seq pipelines on the same FASTQ dataset must be compared numerically to validate that an alternative pipeline reproduces native-pipeline output. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-count-matrices))
- **pearson-correlation-of-cross-model-scores**: Use when two sequence-based variant-effect models (e.g. DeepFun and DeepSEA) have been run on the same SNP set and you must quantify per-assay score agreement, such as CTCF, DNase-seq, H3K4me3, or H3K9ac concordance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-cross-model-scores))
- **pearson-correlation-of-crup-probability-tracks**: Reference for computing or benchmarking the cor_CRUP generic feature (Pearson correlation between summed CRUP-EP and CRUP-PP probability tracks across cell types) when building or ablation-testing ET-interaction feature vectors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-crup-probability-tracks))
- **pearson-correlation-of-fold-change-signatures**: Use when two differential analysis results (e.g., young-vs-old log2 fold-change per ATAC-seq peak in men vs women) must be compared on a common feature set to measure how much of the response is shared versus sex- or condition-specific. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-fold-change-signatures))
- **pearson-correlation-of-genomic-tracks**: Use when you have CscoreTool-M cscore.txt or cscore.bedgraph sub-compartment probabilities and an independent track (NAD-seq, Lamin-B1 DamID) that can be aggregated over the same 100 kb windows, and you want to quantify agreement. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-genomic-tracks))
- **pearson-correlation-of-pathway-activity-with-clinical-phenotype**: Use when you have a gene expression matrix paired with a continuous clinical variable (e.g. 1-hour OGTT blood glucose) and need to test which of ~400 predefined KEGG/Biocarta pathways' activity levels correlate with the phenotype. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-pathway-activity-with-clinical-phenotype))
- **pearson-correlation-of-peak-areas**: Use when validating a GC×GC–TOFMS peak-picking/quantification workflow against reference software using paired peak-area tables, especially with a serial dilution series (e.g. 1 ppm Breath Mix QC) where both correlation and dilution linearity must be shown. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-peak-areas))
- **pearson-correlation-of-peak-areas-to-mixing-ratios**: Use when evaluating metabolomics peak-area quantification accuracy on a dilution/mixing series with known expected ratios, especially comparing two processing tools' feature tables. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-peak-areas-to-mixing-ratios))
- **pearson-correlation-of-per-protein-background-levels**: Use when a CITE-seq (or ASAP-seq/TEA-seq) ADT dataset has both a raw empty-droplet matrix and a cell-containing matrix, and you need to verify that empty-droplet counts faithfully capture the ambient antibody background before using them for dsb step I correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-per-protein-background-levels))
- **pearson-correlation-of-per-site-methylation-levels**: Use when comparing methylation levels between a targeted/enrichment Nanopore run (e.g., RRMS adaptive sampling) and a whole-genome Nanopore run to quantify concordance of per-site CpG methylation frequencies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-per-site-methylation-levels))
- **pearson-correlation-of-perturbation-ranking-profiles**: Use when comparing perturbation effect ranking lists produced under alternative MUSIC preprocessing settings (imputation on/off, Cell_filtering on/off) to measure ranking-list similarity and identify which strategy shifts the ranking most. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-perturbation-ranking-profiles))
- **pearson-correlation-of-umi-counts**: Use when two aligned gene-barcode matrices (same barcodes, zero-padded genes) from different processing pipelines must be quantitatively compared at the UMI-count level to assess processing concordance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-umi-counts))
- **pearson-correlation-outlier-cell-filtering**: Use when a scChIP-seq or scATAC-seq count matrix has passed read-count QC (e.g. ≥1600 unique reads, top 5% coverage removed) but may still contain isolated low-quality outlier cells before dimensionality reduction and clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-outlier-cell-filtering))
- **pearson-correlation-permutation-significance-testing**: Use when building gene co-expression networks from RNA-Seq expression matrices where Pearson-correlation edges must be filtered for statistical significance before fusing with other similarity-based networks (e.g., Euclidean-distance networks). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-permutation-significance-testing))
- **pearson-correlation-protein-network-inference**: Use when you need a plain co-expression/PCC baseline network on a protein-by-condition matrix (tsv/csv, e.g. HPA 566,109-cell scRNA-seq or PRIDE bulk proteomics) to benchmark against a learned method like FAVA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-protein-network-inference))
- **pearson-correlation-replicate-reproducibility-analysis**: Use when you have expression quantification from ≥2 sequencing replicates (or two quantification tools on the same samples) and need to verify reproducibility (e.g., replicate Pearson r &gt; 0.971) or cross-tool consistency (e.g., r ≈ 0.83). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-replicate-reproducibility-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-sample-assignment**: Use when each sample in a log2-transformed, median-centered tumor expression matrix (e.g., the SRIQ '_mc_log2_nz' output) must be labeled with a nearest-centroid TRU/PI/PP call to compare against an independent clustering solution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-sample-assignment))
- **pearson-correlation-scatter-visualization**: Use when validating that clustering features (e.g., Spearman correlation features from sscClust) remain robust to subsampling fluctuations across multiple subsampling rates of an scRNA-seq dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-scatter-visualization))
- **pearson-correlation-significance-testing**: Use when you need to quantify and significance-test the association between two per-cell score vectors (e.g., AXL-high vs MITF-high program scores in scRNA-seq) and compare that association before vs after confounder correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-significance-testing))
- **pearson-correlation-significance-testing-by-random-shuffling**: Use when two co-registered LA-ICP-MS elemental images (identical pixel dimensions) require quantification of spatial colocalisation, and a significance probability for the observed Pearson R is needed beyond the raw correlation coefficient. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-significance-testing-by-random-shuffling))
- **pearson-correlation-similarity-assessment**: Use when validating that computationally inferred (semi-profiled) single-cell data reproduces the ground-truth cohort structure, by correlating per-condition cell-type proportion vectors and pathway activation scores between two cohorts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-similarity-assessment))
- **pearson-correlation-spectral-similarity-assessment**: Use when you have generated a simulated 1D or 2D metabolomic 1H NMR spectrum (urine, blood, or CSF) and need an objective, scalar measure of its resemblance to a real experimental spectrum before using the simulation for algorithm development or validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-spectral-similarity-assessment))
- **pearson-correlation-stability-analysis**: Use when a model has a stochastic component (e.g. random GAN initialization) and you must test whether an internal loss reliably predicts external alignment quality across repeated runs, via per-run Pearson correlations between loss and metric trajectories. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-stability-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-statistical-testing**: Use when when you have two co-registered LA-ICP-MS element images and need to determine whether the spatial distribution of one element correlates significantly with another. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-statistical-testing))
- **pearson-correlation-statistics**: Use when you have paired pairwise dissimilarity values (e.g., MSA-based MSD and phylogenetic branch length BLD) between OTU representative sequences and their member reads, and need to quantify agreement at read vs. OTU level. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-statistics))
- **pearson-correlation-strategy-selection**: Decision support for choosing NeuCA's annotation route when a labelled training scRNA-seq matrix is available: compute cross-cell-type correlations and check whether any off-diagonal element reaches tau = 0.95. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-strategy-selection))
- **pearson-correlation-testing**: Use when interpreting discriminant MOFA (or DIABLO) factors by testing their association with clinical metadata — numeric features via Pearson correlation, binary features via Wilcoxon — inside the BiomiX R pipeline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-testing))
- **pearson-correlation-testing-of-ion-pairs**: Use when an annotated massdiff object of matched adduct ion pairs has been generated from MSI peak data and the candidate parent–adduct pairs must be tested for spatial co-localization before being accepted as true adducts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-testing-of-ion-pairs))
- **pearson-correlation-validation**: Use when a newly proposed clustering evaluation metric must be validated against an established ground-truth evaluation on a dataset where reference results exist, via Pearson correlation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-validation))
- **pearson-correlation-validation**: Use when you have per-cell TCR-contribution scores from a trained mvTCR model plus TCRdist similarities per specificity group, and you must validate that TCR contribution quantitatively tracks TCR sequence similarity across epitope groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-validation))
- **pearson-logistic-gsea-baseline-execution**: Use when a new TR–drug response inference method (e.g. RePhine) must be compared fairly against established mRNA- and target-based baselines on the same simulated noise or confounder scenarios. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-logistic-gsea-baseline-execution))
- **pearson-neighborhood-similarity-scoring**: Use when you need to decide, cell by cell, whether a 2D embedding (t-SNE or UMAP) faithfully preserves pre-embedding neighborhood structure, or when optimizing hyperparameters like t-SNE perplexity by minimizing dubious embeddings. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-neighborhood-similarity-scoring))
- **pearson-r2-accuracy-evaluation**: Use when benchmarking a taxonomic classifier or compositional estimator against known mock/simulated community ground truth and you need quantitative correlation (Pearson r2) and error (L1) metrics on abundance profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/pearson-r2-accuracy-evaluation))
- **pearson-residual-computation-zinb**: Use when analyzing single-cell scRNA-seq count data (e.g. the pancreatic islet dataset with individual/sample IDs) to decide which genes require random intercepts before a full TWO-SIGMA mixed-model fit. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pearson-residual-computation-zinb))
- **pearson-residual-matrix-computation**: Use when you have a raw scRNAseq count matrix and need the chi-squared residual matrix for CA dimension reduction, or when verifying a re-implementation of the corral_preproc count-to-residual pre-processing stage element-wise. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-residual-matrix-computation))
- **pearson-spearman-matrix-computation**: Use when a named-column catalog (e.g. galaxy semi-analytic mock data or SDSS supernova host properties) needs pairwise variable-similarity matrices to feed hierarchical clustering under different correlation choices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/pearson-spearman-matrix-computation))
- **pearsoncorrcoef**: Compute the PearsonCorrCoef metric — provided by torchmetrics. Use when the user has predictions and ground-truth and needs to compute PearsonCorrCoef, or asks how to score with PearsonCorrCoef. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pearsoncorrcoef) · ★3)
- **pearsonr**: Compute the pearsonr metric — provided by scipy.stats. Use when the user has predictions and ground-truth and needs to compute pearsonr, or asks how to score with pearsonr. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pearsonr) · ★3)
- **pearsonscontingencycoefficient**: Compute the PearsonsContingencyCoefficient metric — provided by torchmetrics. Use when the user has predictions and ground-truth and needs to compute PearsonsContingencyCoefficient, or asks how to score with PearsonsContingencyCoefficient. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pearsonscontingencycoefficient) · ★3)
